- Queue at checkout: **3 open findings, all `class: safe-fix`, all `status: open` with `branch:` blank and no `escalate:` key** — QA-20260827-04 (`/cv/<token>` self-fill survey has no photo-upload control), QA-20260828-02 (seven `<Link>` entry points share the `/quotes/new` render-time-write hazard fixed at only one call site), QA-20260828-03 (commercial quote still renders the residential accept flow at `/q/<token>`). Never attempted, i.e. first-pass (Fable) work by the tier boundary, not escalation-tier work. Open PRs on the repo: **1**, `#161` (`gate10b/commercial-office`, label `pitmans-gate`) — no `qa-repair` PR in flight from either tier.
- **The "queue can only grow" alarm of 08-28T00:33Z/06:33Z is resolved — but not by the repair loop.** The 11-deep queue those sweeps stepped around was cleared between 08-28T15:34Z and 16:20Z by interactive human sessions (`627c096` #150 three safe-fix findings, `96438f6` #148, `5291f03` #149, `6b91915` #151, `4af532b` #153, then `0485286` closing 9 at verify-first). Good news, recorded as such; it does not mean the automated repair half recovered.
- **First-pass tier still has not fired.** Last first-pass entry of any kind in this log remains **2026-08-26T05:35Z** — now **~67 hours**. Since then the escalation tier has fired three more times (08-28T00:33Z, 06:33Z, this run) and first-pass has logged nothing, and a first-pass sweep that finds nothing eligible still logs. Three safe-fix findings have arrived and sat untouched in that window. The escalation tier stays a structural no-op while this holds: it can only take what first-pass hands it.
- **New this sweep — a trap in the queue, flagged not acted on.** `QA-20260827-04` carries `class: safe-fix` in its frontmatter, but its own `## Triage (2026-08-28)` section concludes the opposite in bold: the render gate is not the only blocker (every server action the photo widget calls is `requireOfficeProfile()`-gated and a `/cv/<token>` visitor has no office session), it needs a service-role customer action plus a token-scoped find-or-create for the `surveys` row, it is "roughly half a day", and it should be "reclassif[ied] as a build item rather than leaving it in the repair queue, where its class label promises a fix the queue cannot deliver." The reclassification was written but never applied to the frontmatter. So whenever first-pass does come back, `QA-20260827-04` is the oldest safe-fix in the queue and it will claim it and burn a run on a finding the tier cannot finish. One-line fix (flip `class:` and move it to the build backlog); left to Peter because reclassifying another tier's finding is not this tier's mandate. Same caveat likely applies to QA-20260828-02, which the 08-28T20:xxZ audit entry also describes as triaged out to a build item but which carries no triage section and no changed label.
- Deliberately **not** self-authorized, same reasoning as 08-24, 08-28T00:33Z and 08-28T06:33Z: fixing an untried `safe-fix` here would violate `qa/REPAIR.md` §2 and dissolve the tier boundary the two-model pipeline depends on; re-tagging findings `escalate: opus-5` would be manufacturing my own work order. All three left exactly as filed.
- Findings taken: **none**. PRs opened: **none**. Escalations written: **none**. Peter notified.
- Time spent: ~7 min.

## 2026-08-24T~09:25Z — Peter-directed: role agents moved to sonnet; QA-20260823-05 and -06 fixed, verified live, CLOSED

- Tier: interactive ("update those models from haiku to sonnet and correct the issues... before pushing live"). Pulled to `ce98645`, worked in an isolated worktree. [PR #71](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/71) → staging `68439b5`, full pipeline green (changes/test/deploy-staging/e2e, **131 e2e passed**).
- **`AUDIT.md`: role agents now run on `sonnet`, never `haiku`**, with the 04:32Z evidence recorded inline so a future run does not re-derive "cheaper is better". Added beyond the tier bump (flagged to Peter, strip if unwanted): a role agent's report is evidence **only** if it carries literal automation artifacts — the `page.url()` trail it visited and the SQL read-back it ran — and the main loop spot-checks one claim per agent every run, because self-reported cleanup was the claim that failed first. The reasoning: a confident narrator is not a tier-specific failure, so the model change alone does not close the hole.
- **QA-20260823-05 (medium) — FIXED, VERIFIED, CLOSED.** The teardown deleted `leads` while the `communications` and `activities` rows the tested flow itself creates still referenced them; those FKs carry no `ON DELETE` action, so Postgres refused with `23503`, the `clients` delete behind it never ran, and no call checked `error`. Now deletes those children first — and stops being silent: every error is collected, the lead is **read back** to prove it is gone (a delete that removed nothing returns no error either), and anything left **throws** with Postgres's own constraint name attached. A red pipeline is the correct outcome for a teardown that cannot finish; the alternative is what happened, unbounded growth behind a green tick.
  - Verified by the check the finding implies: CI run 32709652995 ran the h8 spec (tests 95 + 96 green), and a service-role query immediately after returned **0** leaked leads and **0** stray comms. Every prior run left exactly one set.
  - Purged the set still present in staging beforehand — **1**, from the 08:52Z CI run, not the 4 the finding recorded, because the 20:07Z audit had already swept the earlier ones (its own log entry says so). The leak was per-run and ongoing, not historical.
- **QA-20260823-06 (low) — FIXED, VERIFIED, CLOSED.** `slotMinTime`/`slotMaxTime` were pinned 07:00–20:00, so a booking outside those hours was absent from Week and Day view with no "+N more" and no scroll cue — in the two views the office allocates crew from. New `lib/schedule/slot-range.ts` derives the window from the events actually rendered: it starts at the same working range and stretches only far enough to include what is booked, so an out-of-hours job pulls the grid open instead of falling off it. A fixed 00:00–24:00 was rejected — that trades a silent omission for a permanently sparse grid. Reads `shown` not `events` (a survey hidden by the removals filter must not stretch the grid for a job you cannot see); hours read in `Europe/London` not `getHours()`, so it is correct in BST and deterministic in tests. **8 tests, mutation-verified: reinstating the hard-coded window fails 5 of 8.**
  - Live-verified on deployed staging with a seeded 21:30 UK removal: **Week 1 visible, Day 1 visible** (toolbar confirmed on the seeded date), Month unchanged, grid stretched to a 23:00 bound. Before the fix both time-grid views rendered zero.
  - **Method correction worth recording:** the first Day-view pass reported 0 events, which was my navigation and not the app — `getByRole("button", {name:"Day"})` and the "next" control did not resolve, so the view never reached the seeded date. Re-run against FullCalendar's own `.fc-timeGridDay-button` / `.fc-next-button` with a toolbar-title assertion before counting. A zero produced by a broken locator is precisely the shape that gets mistaken for evidence, so it is reported rather than dropped.
- Findings filed: none new. `qa/findings/open/` is empty. Specs added: none — QA-06's logic is unit-tested and mutation-verified at the boundary, which is cheaper and more durable than an e2e that would need an out-of-hours fixture on every run; QA-05's fix IS a spec change and is proven by the spec's own CI run.

## 2026-08-23T~19:45Z — Peter-directed: QA-20260823-04 fixed, verified live, CLOSED

- Tier: interactive ("continue with 04"). Pulled clean to `e146ad5`; worked in an isolated worktree.
- **The finding under-stated its own defect, and the fix is the larger one.** QA-20260823-04 filed the stale copy on `/my-jobs` — *"you're put on a job — or taken off one"* — left behind when `bd58fa0`/#56 retired the `crew_job` push category. That copy is real, but reading the registry showed the whole panel is inert for its only audience: all four surviving categories are `admin`/`estimator`, and `getPushConfigAction` filters by role, so a crew member who taps **Enable notifications** spends the browser's one-shot permission prompt, lands on an empty "What you receive" list, and receives nothing, forever, silently. A *denied* prompt is close to unrecoverable, so asking for permission that delivers zero today is pure downside risk against any future real crew push. Job sheets reach crew by email + SMS (`lib/crew-sheet/dispatch.ts`), never push — there was genuinely nothing left to promise.
- **Fix ([PR #66](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/66), squashed to `bef67c5`).** New `pushCategoriesForRole(role)` in `lib/push/categories.ts` is the single source of truth for both the preference list and whether to offer the opt-in at all; `app/actions/push.ts` uses it (the audience filter was duplicated inline, under a comment still claiming *"crew see job assignments"*); `NotificationsRow` renders `null` when the viewer's role receives nothing and no longer carries a hardcoded promise of its own — `NotificationsSetup` already states accurate copy for every state. **The gate is `pushCategoriesForRole(role)`, not `role !== "crew"`**, so the entry point returns by itself the day a crew-audience category is added instead of depending on someone remembering this file exists — which is exactly the rot that produced the finding.
- **TDD + mutation-verified.** Three new tests, RED confirmed before the fix. Adding `crew` to any category's audience fails the guard (2 tests), so the premise cannot change silently.
- **Verified live on deployed staging `bef67c5`, both directions.** Two throwaway logins, created for the check and deleted after.
  - Case A (the finding): crew `/my-jobs` renders the "Your device" section normally — section present, quick sign-in present, user-manual link present — and the opt-in is **gone**: 0 matches for "Get alerts on this phone", 0 for the old "Get job alerts on this phone", 0 for `/put on a job/i`.
  - Case B (regression guard): an admin on the **same page and build** still sees the row and it expands — proving the gate reads the registry rather than blanket-removing the panel. Its copy now comes from `NotificationsSetup` itself, with no job-assignment promise anywhere.
- **Recorded rather than glossed:** `business_settings.push_enabled=false` on staging (the master kill switch, pre-existing and unrelated), so Case B showed the `admin_disabled` state and the "What you receive" list itself could not be observed. That does not touch what was under test — the gate is computed from role before any config is fetched — but "could not check" must not read as a full pass.
- **Specs added:** `e2e/crew/push-optin.spec.ts` — the unit test guards the *cause* (no category names crew), this guards the *wiring* (the page passes the role, the component gates on it). It asserts the surrounding "Your device" items are present in the same breath, so a blank page cannot masquerade as a pass. Also corrected `e2e/COVERAGE.md`'s handoff-h8 row, which still described a `test.skip` that #54/#61 removed — the same stale-record class as this finding.
- **Cleanup:** verified zero in ONE pass this time (events_log by actor, staff, profiles, auth.users), with delete errors surfaced rather than swallowed into a false CLEAN — the failure mode that cost three passes on QA-20260823-03.
- Findings filed: none new. `qa/findings/open/` is now **empty**; GitHub issue #64 closes on this push.
- Pushes: PR #66 (fix) and this commit (evidence + ledger + spec). **master untouched; promotion of staging→master remains Peter's call — it is now 15 commits ahead.**

- No permanent spec added for this flow: the Complete path is a money-settling action and a recurring CI run would settle a seeded refund on every push. The decision logic is unit-tested and mutation-verified instead; this log entry is the record that the UI wiring was proven live once, on the deployed build.
- Also noted: the 16:08Z scheduled audit filed **QA-20260823-04** (safe-fix/low) — the crew push opt-in panel on `/my-jobs` still promises job-assignment alerts that PR #56 retired. Direct fallout of that change; untouched here.
- Pushes: this commit. **master untouched; promotion remains Peter's call.**

## 2026-08-22T~21:30Z — Peter-directed pass: the findings loop was misidentifying its own issues; both remaining risky findings fixed

- Tier: interactive, on Peter's instruction ("deal with any outstanding issues in github from the scheduled audit"). Nothing to pull — local `staging` was already at `origin/staging` `6a785ed`.
- **Defect in the QA loop itself, found while triaging: `qa-findings.yml` identified issues by SEARCH RANK, not by id.** Both jobs looked an issue up with `gh issue list --search "$id in:title"` and took `.[0]`. GitHub scores that across every title *containing* the string, and a finding whose summary cites another finding carries the cited id in its own title. [QA-20260822-03]'s title contains the literal `QA-20260822-02`, outranked the real match, and the 18:02Z close job shut **#44** while leaving **#41** — a finding fixed, merged and CI-verified — reading as an open defect.
  - The close direction is the cosmetic half. **In `raise` the same collision means `number` is non-empty, which the script reads as "already raised" — so a genuine new finding would be logged as handled and never filed.** Same shape as the standing rule that a check which cannot identify its subject must not report success.
  - Fixed by filtering to an exact `[$id]` title prefix. The id is passed through `env` rather than spliced into the jq string, keeping the filter single-quoted (no shell escaping to get wrong), and it runs in gh's **embedded** jq so the workflow gains no external dependency — the first attempt used `jq -r --arg`, which is not installed on this box and would only have been discovered on the runner.
  - Verified against the live API across all six live finding ids **before** merge: identical to the old lookup on five, `#41` instead of `#44` on the one that broke. Then verified end-to-end in CI — [run 32597…](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/actions) on the next `qa/findings/**` push closed `#40` and `#41` correctly and stopped re-closing `#44`. [PR #48](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/48).
- **QA-20260822-01 — VERIFIED and closed.** The finding names its own closing condition (a regression test asserting `assembleJobSheetData(...).moveDate === starts_at` where appointment and quote disagree). That test exists at `tests/lib/job-sheet.test.ts:271-296`, covers the divergent case (23 Aug vs 5 Sept), and passes 21/21; it was recorded failing 2/4 pre-fix, so it is a real guard and not a tautology. One unit test settles all three reported surfaces because they are three *callers of one function*: `lib/job-sheet-load.ts:157` and `lib/crew-sheet/daily-data.ts:170` are the only non-test callers, re-confirmed by grep on the commit. **Not claimed:** no live prod job with divergent dates was re-read, and prod does not carry the fix. [PR #49](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/49).
- **QA-20260819-01 (risky) — FIXED, not closed.** Peter's call: the lead owns its own details; write through to the shared `clients` row only when this lead is the sole enquiry on it.
  - Beyond what the finding recorded: the client-first precedence was hand-written at **six** call sites, one being the Edit dialog's own `initial` values. The dialog seeded phone and email from the shared client, so saving lead B for any unrelated reason wrote lead A's phone and email into **lead B's own `leads` row** — the columns comms sends to. So the damage was never only cosmetic.
  - Both rules extracted to `lib/leads/shared-client.ts`; every site reads one resolved `contact`. Removing the duplication IS the fix — six copies of a precedence rule is how the page came to disagree with itself.
  - Declining the write is **not silent**: `clients.email` is a live sending surface (storage invoices, `raise-storage-invoices.ts:560`), so the action returns `{ ok: true, warning }` and the dialog raises `toast.warning` naming how many other enquiries share the customer. A **failed** sibling count declines the write too, rather than coercing to 0.
- **QA-20260820-08 (risky) — FIXED, not closed.** Peter's call among the finding's three options: count by `client_id`, refuse only the LAST lead on the client. Reasoning: the let always survives a lead delete (`client_id` NOT NULL, client not deleted), so what is protected is the *enquiry behind it*, which a surviving sibling already preserves; blocking every lead would make a typo duplicate undeletable because a sibling has storage. `storageLets` renamed `orphanedStorageLets` — the old name is what let a dead check read as correct. Also made the sibling read in `deleteLeadAction` **fail closed**, which it did not: an ignored error read as "no siblings", which would skip this guard AND lose the history the delete is meant to merge.
- **Both risky fixes are mutation-verified**, not just green: reinstating the exact pre-fix behaviour fails **5 of 9** new shared-client tests and **2 of 15** deletable tests; restoring passes 9/9 and 15/15.
- Findings filed: none. Specs added: none — the two risky fixes were made unit-testable by extraction instead, which is cheaper and more durable than an e2e for a precedence rule, and directly removes the duplication that caused the bug.
- **Both risky findings stay `fixed-pending-verify` in `qa/findings/open/`, so #18 and #31 stay open.** Their Verify steps are live checks the unit tests cannot stand in for — the tests pin the *policy*, not that the action passes the right `client_id` or that the page renders `contact`. Next audit owns both.
- **CI went red on the merge — not on the app.** `office/quotes.spec.ts` hit a strict-mode violation: `getByRole("link", { name: /New quote/i })` resolved to **three** elements, because the /quotes list renders one link per quote whose accessible name *begins with the customer's name*, and staging holds two customers literally called "New quote" (refs `TEST-1787430443670`, `DATETEST-`). Nothing in the change caused it and nothing in the app is wrong — but the suite gates promotion, and a page's primary action identifiable only by data we do not control was always going to break eventually. Matched exactly instead; **Playwright's own failure output named the disambiguation** (`aka getByRole("link", { name: "New quote", exact: true })`), so it is the tool's answer rather than a guess.
  - `estimator/journey.spec.ts` carried the identical latent shape and had not failed: its cockpit also lists assigned rows by customer name. Deliberately NOT fixed with `exact` there — each quick-action card's accessible name absorbs its description line ("New quote Start from customer details"), so exact matching would have turned a passing spec red. Scoped to the "Start a workflow" region instead, which holds only the three quick actions.
  - `office/clients.spec.ts:28` shares the pattern but is a single-customer page whose quote rows are named by ref; left alone rather than changed speculatively.
  - The two junk staging customers were left in place: pre-existing pollution of unknown provenance, harmless once the locators are precise, and deleting staging rows was not in scope. [PR #51](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/51).
- Pushes: PRs #48, #49, #50, #51 — all to `staging` only. **master untouched; promotion remains Peter's call.** Staging verified serving the merged sha via `/api/version`.
- No staging or prod DATA was created or altered at any point this pass — every change is code, tests, workflow or finding files.
- **CI GREEN after the locator fix** — run [32597759777](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/actions/runs/32597759777) on `fcb5c9f`: `test`, `deploy-staging`, `e2e` all success, **130 passed (6.0m)**. Both touched specs confirmed individually (`✓ 91 office/quotes.spec.ts`, `✓ 118 estimator/journey.spec.ts`) rather than inferred from the total.
- Time spent: ~2h wall clock (triage + workflow fix + live-API proof ~35 min; the two risky fixes incl. extraction, mutation-testing and four gates ~55 min; e2e diagnosis + fix + two CI cycles ~30 min).

## 2026-08-22T~19:00Z — Peter-directed pass: QA-20260821-01 fixed (risky, authorised), enquiry-push monitor built, MMR089 resolved

- Tier: interactive, on Peter's explicit instruction ("fix this" / "do it"). Read-only against prod and Sanity throughout; no prod data was altered.
- **QA-20260821-01 (risky/high) — FIXED and merged.** `class: risky` normally puts a finding on the Peter-only path; Peter authorised it directly, which is what took it off that path. `app/api/documents/contract/[signatureId]/route.ts` now guards with `requireOfficeProfile()` returning **403**, and the check runs BEFORE the signature lookup so the reply is identical whether or not the id exists. Cause re-derived rather than trusted: `requireUserOrCronSecret` only *looks* role-blind (it delegates to `requireOfficeOrCronSecret` and is office-gated); a sweep of all **17** `app/api/**/route.ts` using `createAdminClient()` confirms this was the only role-blind one. Two test layers, both proven to fail first: a new `tests/lib/security/api-route-guards.test.ts` that catches the NEXT such route at test time (3/4 failed before), and the audit's skipped `e2e/crew/access.spec.ts` placeholder un-skipped. That placeholder wanted the seed to expose a contract signature id — it needs none: the gate runs before the lookup, so an ABSENT uuid separates the states (pre-fix 404, post-fix 403) with no seeding and without pointing at a real customer's contract. As written it would also have passed vacuously on an empty id. [PR #45](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/45), merged (`8c88f27`).
- **Enquiry-push monitor built.** [PR #46](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/46), merged (`b5e31c0`). Closes the gap this morning's pass flagged: the instant `/api/ingest/lead` push is fail-soft with the Sanity pull as backstop, so a broken push loses nothing, errors nothing, and silently degrades arrival from sub-second to up to 84 minutes — a push that never happened leaves no trace in this database by definition. The signal therefore comes from a channel the fault cannot touch: the site's own `leadDelivery` audit doc in Sanity, whose own header called it "a write-only audit doc with no reader in any codebase". This is now its reader, inside the `sanity-leads-sync` cron and reported separately from the sync so neither can mask the other. `skipped` counts as a fault (past the floor it means the site LOST its config — the exact silent regression), as does a missing `opsIngest` key. A go-live floor stops the dormant era alerting forever, and a garbled override falls back rather than widening the window. **"I could not check" is its own operational issue under a separate key, never a clean result**, and the 200-doc cap is logged when hit so a bounded look never reads as full coverage. The GROQ projects away name/email/phone/postcodes. 14 unit tests using the real production delivery as a fixture; smoke-tested read-only against live Sanity (floor correct, PII confirmed absent, the historical dormant doc correctly `fault=true, afterFloor=false → ALERTS=false`).
- **MMR089 resolved — Peter: it is a 2-day move.** So both dates were real and the diary was right all along (`27 Aug 09:00 → 28 Aug 17:00`, quote `days=2`). The remaining defect is narrower: `quotes.moving_date` holds the **END** day. Checked all four accepted multi-day moves on prod — MMR093, MMR098 and MMR090 all store the START; **MMR089 is the only one that doesn't**, so this is one-off data entry, not a convention problem. Worth recording what the pre-fix bug actually meant here: the job sheet showed the quote's date, i.e. **day two of a two-day move**, so crew would have been told to arrive on the second day. Left for a human deliberately — editing `moving_date` on a live accepted quote also moves `balance_due_date` and the chase/commitment timing, and may fire a date-change email, and the customer needs ringing anyway because her PDF says the 28th. [ClickUp 869enmbrg](https://app.clickup.com/t/869enmbrg) updated with the full picture.
- Gates: QA-20260821-01 — lint 0 · tsc 0 · vitest **1857** · build green. Monitor — lint 0 · tsc 0 · vitest **1867** · build green (re-run after a late change so the reported numbers match the shipped code).
- Note on process: neither PR carried the `qa-repair` label, so the auto-merge workflow correctly skipped both — that workflow refuses risky paths by design, and an auth change is exactly what it is meant to refuse. Merged deliberately on Peter's instruction instead. Staging CI runs on push, not on PRs, so the local four gates were the pre-merge evidence.
- **Then QA-20260822-03 had to be fixed too, because it was holding the security fix off prod.** `master` branch protection requires the **`e2e`** check (`required_checks: ["e2e"]`, confirmed via the API), so a red suite meant QA-20260821-01 could sit on staging indefinitely while the contract-PDF leak stayed open on production. Not scope Peter asked for; fixed because the fix he DID ask for was not actually delivered until it could ship. Both failures turned out to be **spec** bugs, matching the 12:09 audit having proven the same handoff green by hand. (1) The office readback could only ever have found 0 rows: `office-statements-view` renders lines only once a card is EXPANDED (`expanded` starts empty, `{l.description}` sits inside the `isOpen` branch), and the spec filtered on the description — markup not yet in the DOM. The human audit passed because it matched crew name + total, which the collapsed card does show. Now locates on name + total, then **expands and asserts the description anyway** — the handoff's whole point is an identical read-back, so the assertion is reached rather than dropped. (2) `hours-to-admin-statements` sorts first, creates this week's invoice and SUBMITS it, leaving `invoicing-submit-lines` — which documents relying on starting statement-free — with no draft to open; with `workers: 1` that is deterministic ordering, not a race. Fixed by making the spec that submits an invoice put it back (new `deleteCrewStatements` teardown), NOT by loosening the sibling to tolerate a submitted statement. Also corrected that spec's header, which claimed it skips in CI for want of `E2E_CREW_PASSWORD`/`E2E_OFFICE_PASSWORD` — CI has both, which is exactly why it ran unvalidated and failed first time out. `job-detail.spec.ts` left alone as the separately-tracked flake. [PR #47](https://github.com/redbananastudios/marley-ops/pull/47), merged (`403f310`). Gates: lint 0 · tsc 0 · vitest **1871** · build green.

Each previously-failing spec verified individually (25 office readback, 27 invoicing draft, 28 job-detail), and **test 20 `cannot fetch the office-only contract PDF (QA-20260821-01)` passed against the DEPLOYED staging app** — the security fix proven live, not merely by inspection, with the rest of the crew access matrix passing alongside so office access was not broken in the process. The tracked `job-detail` flake did not flake this run; that is not evidence it is fixed and it stays tracked.
- **Findings closed on that evidence** (each finding's own `## Verify` was the script CI ran): QA-20260821-01, QA-20260822-02, QA-20260822-03 moved `open/` → `closed/` with the run linked. **QA-20260822-01 deliberately stays `fixed-pending-verify`** — its Verify wants a live check that `/my-jobs/[id]`, the PDF and the day-sheet all show the appointment's date on a job where the two dates diverge, and nothing in this e2e run asserts that. - Health gate on the untouched tree: lint 0 errors (36-warn baseline) · tsc 0 · vitest 1846 passed/7 skipped · build green.
- **QA-20260822-02 (CI red, self-perpetuating) — FIXED, and verified green in CI.** `scripts/seed-e2e.mjs` `wipe()` deleted `surveys` before the `appointments` referencing them via `appointments.survey_id` (a second FK, NO ACTION), and `die()`'s `process.exit(1)` meant the offending row survived to break every later run. Reordered + the remaining `survey_id` links are released first, so the abort is impossible rather than unlikely. Test `tests/scripts/seed-e2e-wipe-order.test.ts` pins the ordering by reading the script's source (wipe() needs a live DB); **verified to fail 3/3 before, pass 3/3 after**. PR #42, auto-merged. **Verification: staging run 32585752443 — "Seed staging DB: completed/success"**, and the Playwright step executed for the first time instead of being skipped. **The e2e JOB is still red**, but for a different reason and one layer down: with the specs finally able to run, **3 crew specs fail** (`hours-to-admin-statements` office readback, `invoicing-submit-lines`, and the already-tracked `job-detail` flake). They were hidden for three CI runs behind the seed abort, and the seed fix touches no statements/invoicing code — so this is what was already underneath, not a regression. Filed as **QA-20260822-03** with two competing hypotheses and deliberately no fix: the run uploaded trace artifacts, and those should be read before either spec is touched. Do not report CI as green until that finding closes.
- **QA-20260822-01 (crew told the wrong day) — FIXED, pending live verify.** `lib/job-sheet-data.ts` now prefers the appointment's `starts_at` over the quote's stale `moving_date`. Deliberately **not** a `.slice(0, 10)`: that takes the UTC day while `/my-jobs` buckets by `Europe/London`, so the naive swap would have traded the reported divergence for a quieter BST one. New `ukCalendarDate()` in `lib/uk-time.ts`. Four cases added to `tests/lib/job-sheet.test.ts` (live repro, no-drift, undated fallback, BST midnight edge); **verified to fail 2/4 before, 21/21 pass after**. Gates: lint 0 · tsc 0 · vitest 1850 · build green. PR #43, auto-merged (`115559c`). Live verification of the crew surfaces is the next audit's job — the `## Verify` block is its script.
- **Live impact of QA-20260822-01, found by a read-only prod sweep**: exactly one real job carries diverged dates — appointment `c38d78fc`, **Janet Jones MMR089**, diary **Thu 27 Aug 09:00** vs accepted quote **28 Aug**, `scheduled`, ~5 days out, **0 crew assigned** so no day-sheet has carried the wrong date to anyone. The code fix makes ops self-consistent but cannot decide which date is TRUE — the customer's quote PDF says the 28th. Filed for Luke/Connor: [ClickUp 869enmbrg](https://app.clickup.com/t/869enmbrg).

Also documented: `gh` CLI is not installed on this host (used GitHub MCP `actions_list`/`actions_get` instead) and a `chromium_headless_shell` version mismatch against the pre-installed browsers (use `executablePath: /opt/pw-browsers/chromium`).
